The Secretary of Health and Human Services has determined that the publication of this 
periodical is necessary in the transaction of the public business required by law of this De- 
partment. Use of funds for printing this periodical has been approved by the Director of the 
Office of Management and Budget through May 31, 1987. 
 
ENVIRONMENTAL HEALTH PERSPECTIVES is a publication of the National 
Institute of Environmental Health Sciences, National Institutes of Health, Department of 
Health and Human Services. Opinions expressed herein, however, are not necessarily 
endorsed nor shared by any of the above agencies.

Instructions to Authors 
 
Environmental Health Perspectives (EHP) is published by the 
National Institute of Environmental Health Sciences. The objectives 
of EHP are to communicate research findings of environmental health 
significance and to inform the scientific community of potential health 
hazards that are associated with particular elements in the environ- 
ment. Environmental Health Perspectives publishes conference and 
workshop proceedings, perspective statements on selected problem 
areas, toxicologic information summaries, overviews of areas on en- 
vironmental health, and reviews on specific environmental problems 
and agents. 
 
Articles will be selected by the Editors on the basis of quality and 
pertinence. Only manuscripts prepared in English will be considered. 
All unsolicited manuscripts will be reviewed by members of our Ed- 
itorial Review Board; other manuscripts will be reviewed at the dis- 
cretion of the Editors. Letters commenting upon articles in EHP or 
items of general interest will also be considered. 
 
Submission of Manuscripts 
 
Manuscripts are of two types: full-length and brief reports. Brief 
reports are usually fewer than 1000 words in length. 
 
Manuscripts must be typewritten copy, double-spaced on 8'/2 x 11 
inch paper and must be submitted in triplicate including three copies 
of each figure in addition to one set of original photographs or draw- 
ings. Submit manuscripts to: 
 
Assistant Managing Editor 
 
ENVIRONMENTAL HEALTH PERSPECTIVES (EHP) 
National Institute of Environmental Health Sciences 
P.O. Box 12233 
 
Research Triangle Park, NC 27709 
 
Organization of Manuscript 
 
Organization of the manuscript depends upon the nature of pre- 
sentation and is left to the authors' discretion. However, a running 
title, key words and an abstract must accompany each manuscript. 
Please use the following guidelines for preparing the various sections 
of your manuscript: 
 
Title Page: List title, authors (first or second name spelled out in 
full), complete name of institution or laboratory where work was done, 
and affiliation of each author. Indicate author to whom galley proofs 
and reprints should be sent (including telephone number). 
 
Running Head. In addition to the main title, provide a short title 
(not to exceed 60 characters and spaces) to be used as a running head. 
List this information on the title page. 
 
Key Words: On a separate sheet, list 5-10 key words to be used for 
indexing purposes. Also list on this sheet the names of the authors 
and the title of the paper. 
 
Abstract: Begin this section on a separate page. The abstract should 
be 200-250 words in length. 
 
Acknowledgements: Begin this section on a separate page. Financial 
support and technical assistance should be listed in this section. 
 
References: Begin this section on a separate page. References are 
to be numbered in order of citation in the text and should be cited in 
 
the text in parentheses. The style for references to journals or books 
in the bibliography is as follows: 
 
Journal Article 
 
Viola, P. L., Biogotti, A., and Caputo, A. Oncogenic response 
to rat skin, lungs and bones to vinyl chloride. Cancer Res. 31: 
516-522 (1971). 
Book Chapter 
 
Lohman, A. H. M., and Lammers, A. C. On the structure and 
fiber connections of the olfactory centres in mammals. In: Prog- 
ress in Brain Research: Sensory Mechanisms, Vol. 23 (Y. Zot- 
terman, Ed.), Elsevier, New York, 1967, pp. 65-82. 
Book 
 
Harper, R., Smith, E. C. B., and Land, D. B. Odour Descrip- 
tion and Classification. American Elsevier, New York, 1968. 
Editor as Author 
 
Doty, R. L., Ed. Mammalian Olfaction, Reproductive Pro- 
cesses, and Behavior. Academic Press, New York, 1976, p. 
337. 
 
Abbreviate journal names according to Chemical Abstracts or 
Biological Abstracts List of Serials (BIOSIS). List all authors; do not 
use et al. in the bibliography. Include title of journal article or book 
chapter and complete pagination. Reference to papers that have been 
accepted for publication but have not yet been published should be 
cited in the same manner as other references, with the name of the 
journal followed by "in press." "Personal Communication" and "Un- 
published Observations" should not be listed in the bibliography. They 
are to be inserted at appropriate places in the text, in parentheses, 
without a reference number. 
 
Footnotes: Footnotes should be avoided whenever possible. How- 
ever, when they are necessary, please use the following guidelines: 
Use unnumbered footnotes to the title page to indicate author affili- 
ations and addresses. Place these footnotes on the title page. Foot- 
notes in text should be numbered in sequence, using superscript num- 
bers. List these numbered footnotes on a separate page following 
references. 
 
Tables: Each table should be on a separate page. Tables should be 
numbered with arabic numerals. The title should be centered and only 
the first word and proper nouns capitalized. Footnotes to the table 
should be indicated by lower case superscript letters, beginning with 
a for each table. Footnotes should be typed directly after the table. 
 
Chemical Structures, Chemical Equations and Mathematical 
Equations: To avoid typesetting problems, structures and equations 
should be submitted as either original drawings or as glossy photo- 
graphic prints. 
 
Figures: Graphs and figures should be submitted either as original 
drawings in black India ink or as glossy photographic prints. Graphs 
and other drawn figures will be reproduced as submitted and will not 
be redrawn. One set of original unmounted glossy photographic prints 
or line drawings must accompany the submitted manuscript. In ad- 
dition, one complete set (copies) of the figures should be attached to 
each copy of the manuscript. The style of figures should be uniform 
throughout the paper. Letters, numbers and symbols must be drawn 
to be at least 1.5 mm high after reduction. Choose a scale so that 
each figure may be reduced to two column width or one column width. 
Identify all figures on the back with authors' names and figure number 
and indicate TOP. 
 
Figure Legends: Figure legends should be typed on a page separate 
from the figure. Legends should be numbered with arabic numerals, 
followed by the title with only the first word and proper nouns cap- 
italized. Explanatory material should be in paragraph form below the 
title. 
 
Abbreviations: Abbreviations used in the text should be defined in 
the text in parentheses when first mentioned. Nomenclature and sym- 
bols should conform to the recommendations of the American Chem- 
ical Society or the International Union of Pure and Applied Chemistry 
(IUPAC). 
 
Galley Proofs, Reprints and Subscriptions 
 
Galley Proofs and Reprints: Galley proofs and reprints will be 
mailed to the first author or the author designated on the title page. 
 
Once the author has received galley proofs, they must be mailed to 
the EHP office within 72 hours. Following publication, 200 free re- 
prints will be mailed to the author. 
 
Subscription Information: The three most recently published issues 
and yearly subscriptions are available from the Superintendent of 
Documents, Government Printing Office, Washington, DC 20402. 
Yearly subscription: $46 in U.S. and $57.50 outside U.S. Single issue: 
$11 in U.S. and $13.75 outside U.S. Back issues are available free of 
charge from the Assistant Managing Editor, EHP, NIEHS, P.O. Box 
12233, Research Triangle Park, NC 27709. Refer to "Published Vol- 
umes of EHP" in the most recent issue to determine which issues are 
available free of charge and which issues are available by subscription.
